| [l1-declarative-configuration.md](specifications/l1-declarative-configuration.md) ; v1.2.0 DC-12 — **silence is a lenient default, an explicit declaration is a strict contract**, and it may not be withdrawn to pass: an omitted optional field lets the owner pick a safe best-effort behaviour and degrade quietly, while the same field declared must be satisfied or rejected with a diagnostic rather than silently served with the omitted-case behaviour (which turns an explicit request into an invisible no-op); declaring therefore **raises** the bar, and dropping a declaration to reach a green result is DC-3's rejection routed around rather than answered (AG-8) | Declarative configuration surfaces — one mechanism for everything configurable, with no editor hand-written for any of it: the owning component publishes the **single** machine-readable declaration of its configuration (fields, types, ranges, defaults, required-ness, secrecy, descriptions) and a second separately-maintained copy anywhere — a hand-written form, a duplicated docs table, a parallel schema — is forbidden because it will drift invisibly (DC-1); every editing surface graphical/terminal/agent-facing is **generated** from the declaration so a new configurable component adds zero editor code, and a component with no declaration is not editable through the generic surface at all rather than editable by guesswork (DC-2); **the owner validates** — a proposed change goes to the owning component before taking effect, shape-validity being necessary and never sufficient since environmental, cross-instance, and semantic validity are structurally unavailable to any editing surface, with a typed explanatory rejection naming the offending field (DC-3); nothing effective until accepted and application atomic, the previously effective configuration remaining fully effective until then so a rejected or failed change never leaves a component unconfigured (DC-4); **three kinds — single, template, instance** — with the available actions determined by kind (a template is instantiated but never enabled; an instance is enabled/disabled/tested/removed), making configure-fifteen-of-the-same-thing a first-class operation instead of a copy-paste discipline (DC-5); durable, attributed (who changed what when), and revertible to the prior version (DC-6); identical mechanism for built-in and extension-supplied components with no privileged internal path (DC-7); stable hierarchical addressing (owner → category → name) used by audit, permissions, and automation, renaming being a recorded migration never a silent re-point (DC-8); secrets write-only through the surface — settable, replaceable, clearable, but never returned by a read, rendered, exported, or traced, honoured automatically by every generated surface (DC-9); permission-gated and authority-preserving — a consequential change passes the same risk-proportional gate as any other effect and configuration can never grant a capability, relax a policy, or raise a limit the authority plane owns (DC-10). Ends at acceptance; l2-config-hotreload owns application. Nodus realization = NL-20 `§config`. Concept-only (tech-agnostic)  ; v1.1.0 adds DC-11 layered resolution with **per-field authority** — each field declares the lowest-trust layer permitted to set it: environment-owned fields (bindings, local tool paths, credentials) are operator-only, a lower-trust value never feeds an exec-adjacent decision, and lower-trust configuration is disclosed rather than silent | Stable | 1 | 1.2.0 |
| [l1-outcome-confidence.md](specifications/l1-outcome-confidence.md) | Outcome confidence — a calibrated, decomposed estimate of the probability that delivered work actually satisfies what was asked, computed at the delivery boundary from the *whole trajectory* rather than the final artifact, as the instrument against **silent incompleteness** (work that ran cleanly, passed every check it chose to run, and still did not do what was asked — the region where no claim was made, so no check can falsify anything): the object of judgement is the outcome read from the trajectory, since the evidence of incompleteness lives in the process (a test never run, a scope silently narrowed, a broken dependency worked around unsaid) not in the deliverable (OC-1); **decomposed into named contributors from a closed declared vocabulary, never a bare scalar** — "68%" is unactionable, "68%, because no test was executed and error handling was never addressed" is a work order (OC-2); **anticipated follow-up as a first-class contributor family** — estimating the specific ways the requester will have to come back is the only question that reaches past the checks that were run to the ones never conceived (OC-3); **calibration as an obligation** — a value shown as a probability is measured against realized outcomes (did the requester have to come back?) and, where calibration is unknown/stale/drifted, is shown as an ordinal or withheld, since an unmeasured percentage borrows the authority of measurement and is trusted more than it deserves (OC-4); **confidence never substitutes for evidence** in the most dangerous direction — a high estimate may never satisfy/waive/shorten a required check, verification deciding *done* while confidence decides *how hard to look and whom to involve* (OC-5); a low estimate carries a declared behavioural consequence (continue on the named contributors / run a specific check / escalate / hand over with uncertainty stated), never merely a hedged sentence, because a decorative signal teaches the user to ignore the channel (OC-6); producer-independent and never self-declared, since producers that are wrong most often sound most certain (OC-7); bounded, degradable, and never default-confident — *unassessed* is a visible state (OC-8); reproducible from a recorded evidence span with assessor identity and per-contributor scores (OC-9); comparable across candidate outcomes but **never aggregated into a reputation score** for a worker/role/person, which would both corrupt the metric (it becomes a target) and the work (it rewards looking confident over being correct) (OC-10). Supplies the IR-8/IR-9 entry signal and revision targets. Concept-only (tech-agnostic) | Stable | 1 | 1.0.0 |
| [l1-environment-lifecycle.md](specifications/l1-environment-lifecycle.md) | Execution environment lifecycle — the lifetime contract for the **ephemeral** environment agent work runs in, deliberately distinct from the sandbox confinement policy (authority — what code may do) and from organizational workspace lifecycle (durable, human-scale offices): specification/instance split so an environment is reproducible from a name and never assembled ad hoc, a specification carrying base template + entry command + initial variables + working root + resource class + the *names* of required secrets (EL-1); a closed observable status set (provisioning · ready · suspended · resuming · failed · gone) where provisioning and resuming expose progress rather than an opaque block and **failed** stays distinct from **gone** (broken-retry-may-help vs over-do-not-wait) (EL-2); **intent accepted before the environment exists** — the request is taken immediately, given a provisional identity, and its intent durably queued, because a blocking provisioning wait spends the human's time and pushes them into re-sending, which manufactures duplicate work (EL-3); **exactly-once, in-order reconciliation** of the provisional identity onto the real one with queued intent surfaced on provisioning failure — losing it, delivering it twice, and leaving a dangling provisional identity are one invariant's three defects (EL-4); **every exit path names its capture owner** — explicit destroy, idle/expiry reap, quota eviction, host crash, failed resume, deliberate abandonment — with ownership following the *power to destroy* rather than the intention to preserve, since work is preserved on the well-tested path and vanishes without an error on the routes nobody enumerated, and the reap (which never reaches the requesting component) is the dominant one by volume (EL-5); residue captured as a **verifiable base-anchored delta** naming its base's source/revision/root, not an opaque blob — cheap enough to be mandatory on every path, reviewable, and applicable elsewhere, three properties an environment snapshot lacks (EL-6); suspension preserves while destruction captures, and a resume restores **the same instance** or reports a failed resume rather than silently substituting a fresh one the caller believes is the old one (EL-7); bounded lifetime with a declared reaper — an instance nothing owns the expiry of is a standing cost and exposure (EL-8); scoped revocable credentials never issued before *ready* and revoked on suspend/fail/destroy, with exposed services enumerated by name so a consumer never guesses an endpoint (EL-9); provisioning secrets injected and never present in the specification, instance record, conversation record, trace, or residue — so a specification is safe to store, share, version, and review (EL-10). Nodus counterpart = l1-nodus-portability LP-18. Concept-only (tech-agnostic) | Stable | 1 | 1.0.1 |
| [l1-staged-rollout.md](specifications/l1-staged-rollout.md) | Staged rollout & progressive enforcement — how a running system changes **itself** without betting everything on being right, the *release* half of self-improvement (the offline optimizer decides which candidate is better; this decides how it reaches real work): one rule — **nothing goes from absent to universal in a single step, and the counterfactual is measured before the change becomes the only path**. Every behaviour change ships behind a named switch restorable without a rebuild, reinstall, or restart of in-flight work — a change undoable only by shipping another version is a bet, not a rollout (SR-1); assignment is a **pure deterministic stateless function** of (switch identity, subject's pinned key), never drawn at random and never stored, giving reproducibility offline, no assignment state to lose or let drift between assigner and reporter, and an always-answerable "why did this get that variant?" (SR-2); switches are mutually independent by salting on switch identity, so two changes at 10% never land on the same 10% and confound each other (SR-3); **monotone under widening** — raising the fraction only adds, so tuning a percentage never re-shuffles subjects and silently destroys the comparability of everything measured on either side of the tuning, plus its temporal half — a decision taken for a unit of work in flight is frozen for that unit's lifetime, so changing or disabling a switch governs future decisions only and no run straddles two arms (a straddling run is attributable to neither and poisons every comparison it enters, so it must be unrepresentable rather than merely discouraged) (SR-4); the bucketing key is pinned and survives identity transitions, a subject changing arm mid-flight corrupting both its experience and every measurement it contributed to (SR-5); explicit human opt-in short-circuits all percentage and targeting rules and is excluded from the comparison population, since a self-selected group is not a sample (SR-6); a **reserved cross-switch holdout** — the only construct answering whether the *accumulation* of everything shipped beat where the system started, a question no individual comparison asks because each baseline is the previous already-changed state, and one that must be reserved in advance since it cannot be reconstructed after the fact (SR-7); every decision returns the **most informative** reason from a closed vocabulary including the negatives — "outside the exposure fraction" says targeting matched and only the dice did not, which "no match" does not, and a rollout that can only say *no* gets turned off rather than fixed (SR-8); local, offline, fail-closed-to-a-declared-safe-value evaluation with bounded observable staleness, no network round trip per decision, and no secret riding in a distributed ruleset (SR-9); **new constraints ratchet observe → warn → enforce** with the warn threshold *derived* from the enforce threshold so the two cannot drift apart — a limit whose would-have-blocked set was never measured has an unknown blast radius and will discover it by breaking something (SR-10); pre-declared frozen stopping rule with independently-tripping guardrails and an honest *undecided* outcome when the declared minimum is not reached, since deciding by watching until the numbers look favourable is a search for a moment, not a measurement (SR-11). Consumers: l1-harness-optimization HX-12, l1-policy-governance PG-10. Nodus counterparts = l1-nodus-portability LP-19 + l1-nodus-observability HO-18. v1.1.0 SR-12 (shadow evaluation) — a **third** introduction mode beside fractional exposure and the ratchet, for a **recognizer** (detector, classifier, grouping rule, scorer — something deciding *what things are* rather than *what happens to them*): run against all live input while producing no user-visible effect, output compared against the incumbent before either replaces the other; shadow answers what a fraction cannot, since a recognizer's value is its findings across *everything* rather than how a fraction of subjects fare, and conversely a fraction leaves a record with two incompatible classifications in it; safe only under two rules — **quarantined output** (findings raise nothing, notify nobody, enter no record read as real) and **no shared identity namespace with the incumbent**, the trap that makes shadow mode dangerous rather than merely useless when done wrong, because a shadow writing into the same identity space makes every finding *already recorded* by promotion time, so the promoted recognizer sees a world where nothing is new and the entire first-occurrence path (notification, assignment, escalation, initial triage) is silently skipped for exactly the findings the change existed to surface — invisibly, permanently for those findings, and indistinguishable from "it found nothing"; new §4.6 three-modes table. Concept-only (tech-agnostic) | Stable | 1 | 1.1.0 |
| [l1-fault-lifecycle.md](specifications/l1-fault-lifecycle.md) | Fault identity & lifecycle — what makes two failures **the same failure**, and what happens to that failure over its life; the layer upstream of reporting (reporting decides whether and how a failure is filed outward, this decides what the thing being filed *is*). Occurrences collapse into a **fault** every surface addresses, never per-occurrence, since per-occurrence presentation makes frequency the loudest signal in the room and buries the fifty distinct problems under the one firing in a loop (FL-1); identity produced by a **named, versioned** grouping configuration that is migrated rather than edited, because changing what "the same failure" means silently re-partitions all history — a fault that splits or merges keeps its name, counts, resolution, and owner while now describing something else, a corruption that raises no error and is therefore never noticed (FL-2); a **layered explainable ladder** from declared identity down through structural location and typed class to a normalized descriptor of last resort, recording which component contributed and which did not and why, with free text deliberately last since interpolated values shatter one fault into thousands (FL-3); declared identity outranking computed — computed grouping is a default for the failures nobody anticipated, never an authority over the ones somebody did (FL-4); merges that keep the absorbed identity **resolvable** so no filed report, link, ticket, note, or automation dangles — a duplicate is visible and a dead reference is not (FL-5); status refined by **substatus** — unresolved as new / ongoing / regressed / escalating, archived as until-escalating / until-condition / permanent — because a flat open-closed model renders "appeared five minutes ago", "scrolled past for a month", and "the fix did not hold" identically, and a list whose entries all look alike is triaged once then ignored (FL-6); **resolution as a version-anchored claim the system checks** — a recurrence at or after the claimed-fixed version reads as *regressed* while an earlier-version recurrence is explicitly not a regression, so a repair that keeps failing stays visible instead of returning as unrelated strangers (FL-7); **escalation re-surfaces a known fault** whose rate rose above its own baseline, because being known must not be a hiding place — a tenfold increase otherwise produces no signal at all since nothing new appeared (FL-8); archival conditional by default with a stated condition for return, suppressing notification but never recording or counting, permanent archival being an explicit attributed choice since a default-permanent archive is indistinguishable from a fix (FL-9); bounded representative occurrence retention (first, latest, a sample between, plus aggregates) so cost scales with faults not occurrences and the loudest fault is not the most expensive to have (FL-10); the recording pipeline's own failures recorded as **visible faults attributed to their source**, never discarded or buried in an operator-only log, since data that fails to become a record is the data whose absence nobody notices and a silently-dropping pipeline reports a world cleaner than the real one (FL-11). Consumers: l1-error-reporting ERR-7, l1-operational-health OH-14, l1-staged-rollout SR-12. Nodus counterpart = l1-nodus-observability HO-19. Concept-only (tech-agnostic) | Stable | 1 | 1.0.0 |
| [l1-reproduction-recipe.md](specifications/l1-reproduction-recipe.md) | Reproduction recipe — a delivered artifact carries **embedded in itself** the complete versioned recipe that produced it, so a holder of only the artifact can re-derive it or change one thing and re-derive a variant; deliberately the opposite placement to data lineage, which stays in the trace (LN-5) and therefore becomes unreachable at the exact moment an artifact is exported, attached, or handed over and acquires an audience that cannot query anything. The recipe travels with every copy, export, and hand-off (RR-1); **three layers or it is a lie** — the request, the components, and the **ambient configuration in force** that affected the outcome without being asked for, the third being the layer designs forget because at production time it is the environment rather than an input, and whose omission yields a recipe that looks complete, replays under different settings, and reports success (RR-2); components by **content identity** with names for legibility only, since names are the part of a system that drifts fastest across the very interval a recipe exists to cross (RR-3); **the producing version recorded and honoured on replay**, or refusal naming the fields it cannot honour — replaying an old recipe under current semantics and presenting the result as a reproduction fails silently, looks successful, and hides the difference from the person who most needs it (RR-4); every influencing component contributing its own fields, since a silent contributor makes the recipe **undetectably** incomplete (RR-5); completeness not configurable and omissions declared present-but-unavailable with a reason, because a quietly short recipe is read as one that had nothing to omit (RR-6); best-effort per field so one failure does not convert a small gap into total loss at the moment of delivery (RR-7); **determinism stated, never implied** — deterministic / deterministic-given-controls / non-deterministic, since a recipe promising exactness it cannot deliver teaches readers to distrust every recipe (RR-8); secrets named-and-redacted rather than carried or silently dropped, so a reader learns a credential participated without learning it and without being misled into thinking none was needed (RR-9); and a recipe explicitly **not** a claim of authenticity — trivially editable, travelling beside a verifiable witness rather than substituting for one (RR-10). Consumers: l1-competitive-execution CE-10, l1-model-runtime MR-15. Nodus counterpart = l1-nodus-observability HO-20. Concept-only (tech-agnostic) | Stable | 1 | 1.0.0 |
| [l1-convergence-gate.md](specifications/l1-convergence-gate.md) | Convergence gate — put the deterministic check **where all paths converge**, not on each path that leads there, so a policy holds regardless of *who or what* produced the change, including producers nobody anticipated: guard the single boundary every change must cross to become real (durable, shared, shipped, installed) rather than each producing path, making coverage **source-independent by construction** — it catches the agent, a human's manual edit, an automation, an import, or a paste identically, closing the class INT-8 can only name (CG-1); the gate is deterministic code that always runs at its boundary, never an instruction a producer may skip, with an advisory layer allowed on top for legibility but never mistaken for the guarantee (CG-2); it **judges the delta a change introduces, not the accumulated state**, since an absolute-state gate blocks every change on pre-existing debt and is rationally disabled wholesale — a regression is blockable, standing debt is surfaced (CG-3); the enforcement moment is one deliberate choice, not the same policy stacked at many moments returning conflicting verdicts with no authority among them (CG-4); a **deferred obligation** lets an earlier event gate a later dependent effect, discharged only on genuine completion of the required action and never on an attempt (CG-5); an obligation left open at scope end is **surfaced, never silently dropped**, since a forgotten obligation is a policy that quietly did not apply, indistinguishable from one satisfied (CG-6); a **recorded, attributed bypass** — never none (a gate without one is removed at the first emergency) and never silent (an invisible bypass is no gate) (CG-7); fail-closed to block and fail-forward to observe (CG-8); the gate lives with the boundary so **every actor who crosses it inherits it**, including those onboarded after it was placed (CG-9); and it composes inline guards rather than replacing them, the inline guard telling a producer *now* and the boundary gate guaranteeing *regardless of producer* (CG-10). The architectural answer to l1-interception-model INT-8/INT-9. Nodus counterpart = l1-nodus-portability LP-20. Concept-only (tech-agnostic) | Stable | 1 | 1.0.0 |
| [l1-review-checkpoint.md](specifications/l1-review-checkpoint.md) ; v1.1.0 RC-11/RC-12 added - **placement and payload**, the two properties RC-1...RC-10 left to whoever wired the checkpoint: where the position is a choice the checkpoint sits at the **latest still-reversible point** so the human is asked once, late, with everything prepared, bounded in both directions (never past the first irreversible/outward/expensive act, which carries RC-6's own gate, and never past a decision the remaining work depends on, since deferring a question past the work it gates answers it by assumption); and the checkpoint MAY lead with a **decision-ready brief** provided the brief **reaches** the artifact, the artifact stays authoritative, and it asserts no property the reviewer cannot check by following the path - sharpening RC-7, which forbids reviewing a claim *instead of* the work, not leading with a brief | Review checkpoint — the *solicited, in-band* human review: a declared point in a work item's lifecycle where progress **halts and the system asks** a human for a bounded decision before the item advances (“checkpoint” here a review station, not a state snapshot). The in-band solicited complement to out-of-band intervention (HI: human acts unasked, system detects), the human-grader case of iterative refinement (IR: automated grader, rubric verdict), and distinct from action-gating (AG: an *effect* gated by consequence tier, permit/deny only). Halt-and-ask, never advance-while-awaiting (RC-1); a **closed decision set with a revise arm** — approve / request-revision / reject — since approve-or-reject discards the common “yes, but change this” and forces substandard work through or a blind restart (RC-2); the producer neither configures nor clears the review of its own work, the actor with the strongest incentive to pass never holding the gate (RC-3, composes SEC-10); **request-revision returns to the same producer with feedback threaded and the prior attempt preserved** — never discarded, restarted, or reassigned by default, because that context is what makes a revision an edit not a rewrite (RC-4, composes IR-1); the revise↔review loop bounded with a declared honest exit so a demanding reviewer is finite (RC-5, composes IR-5); the terminal/expensive/irreversible step checkpointed **by default** with auto-approve an explicit recorded opt-out, friction defaulting on where a wrong output is costliest (RC-6, composes AG); the reviewer shown the **actual artifact, not a claim about it**, or silent incompleteness re-enters at the moment review exists to catch it (RC-7, composes completion-verification); every review attributed and recorded — who, what, what feedback, which attempt, when (RC-8); a held item pausing itself and its dependents but **never the whole office**, since a review that freezes everything gets disabled to keep moving (RC-9); and pending review a **distinct, visible, owned state** so the decision actually gets made (RC-10). Nodus realization = existing l1-nodus-dialog NL-12 pause + NL-14 grader-gated loop (no new dialog surface). Concept-only (tech-agnostic) | Stable | 1 | 1.1.0 |
| [l1-model-adaptation.md](specifications/l1-model-adaptation.md) | Model adaptation — the office's **second plane of learning**: specializing the local model itself to the office's domain as the deep complement to the context-plane improvement it already does (rules/skills/prompts/harness), by distilling its own recorded successful work into a **small separable adaptation over a frozen base**. Two distinct planes never conflated — context-plane change instant/inspectable/edit-reversible, model-plane change distilled/opaque/detach-reversible (MA-1); a **frozen base with a separable delta, never a base mutation**, giving cheap/reversible/composable/attributable specialization where mutating the base gives none of the four and destroys shared-base dedup (MA-2); composable and per-task attachable so one base serves many domains rather than many base copies (MA-3); distilled from the office's **own verified** experience with provenance preserved, since training on unverified output amplifies the office's own errors into its model — the self-training collapse trap (MA-4); **earned and ratified, never automatic and never self-adopted**, with a gate *at least as strict* as the context plane's because an opaque model-plane change is higher-consequence (MA-5, composes PC-1/PC-2/SEC-10); **measured against the base on a held-out set and staged reversibly** (HX-8 acceptance + staged-rollout), never flipped to default on a training-loss number since lower loss measures fit to the shown data which may be the office's mistakes (MA-6); content-addressed/versioned/attested like any model artifact, an unknown adapter being an unverified capability (MA-7); reversible and non-destructive — detach returns exactly to the prior state, a bad adaptation removed by detaching not by training back (MA-8); **changes dispositions (fluency/style/format) not the knowledge horizon or correctness guarantees** and never relaxes a safety/verification gate, measured against ground truth not self-consistency since a more-fluent model is not a more-correct one (MA-9); optional, local-first (training data and adapter stay on-device), never a runtime dependency (MA-10). Realizes the MR-5 optional-adapters field's lifecycle; the model-plane sibling of pattern-codification (context plane). Concept-only (tech-agnostic) | Stable | 1 | 1.0.0 |
| [l1-voice-output.md](specifications/l1-voice-output.md) | Voice output — the output half of the voice loop (text → speech), the symmetric complement to voice input, whose contract centers on the two things engine choice does not solve: **audio is a derived rendering of canonical text, never the source of truth** — editing the waveform as the record, or treating a transcript-of-the-audio as authoritative, is forbidden, since it creates two versions of what the office said with no authority between them (VO-2, composes derived-artifact / reproduction-recipe); and a **voice is impersonation-capable identity data**, not a stylistic parameter. On-device synthesis with no network dependency (VO-1, mirrors VI-1); a voice profile as a **provenance-bound identity artifact** with a recorded origin class — preset / cloned / generated (VO-3); **cloning a real person's voice requires a recorded consent-of-source**, since the system cannot verify ownership and must not pretend to — clone-without-consent is a **blocked** operation, not a silent capability, converting misuse from a default into a deliberate lie on the record (VO-4); synthetic speech **disclosed/watermarked** where it could be mistaken for a genuine recording of a real person, stripping the disclosure to misrepresent forbidden (VO-5); impersonation-without-consent and the prohibited-use classes (fraud, voice-auth bypass, non-consensual content, misleading official communications) an **enforced policy boundary** the managed tier can harden, not a footnote (VO-6, composes policy-governance / security); pluggable engine whose swap changes the sound never the identity/consent guarantees (VO-7, mirrors VI-7); on-device model lifecycle (VO-8, mirrors VI-8/MR-6); speaking an **explicit, gated, interruptible** output mode so the office never speaks unbidden (VO-9, mirrors VI-3, composes action-gating); rendering never mutating the canonical text, retryable and versioned with lineage (VO-10). The identity/consent/disclosure contract and the derived-rendering discipline are core; the synthesis engine, effects, and UI are out of scope. Concept-only (tech-agnostic) | Stable | 1 | 1.0.0 |
| [l1-confidentiality-flow.md](specifications/l1-confidentiality-flow.md) | Confidentiality flow control — the **outbound** half of information-flow control (how sensitive is this, and where may it flow?), the orthogonal dual of the provenance/taint **integrity** axis (how trustworthy is this?) Cronus already tracks; the injection defense is inbound, this is the exfiltration defense outbound. Two axes never collapsed — a single trust label cannot hold a trusted-and-secret credential or an untrusted-and-public injection, the two orthogonal because the two questions are (CF-1); confidentiality an ordered lattice defaulting to **most-restrictive**, the don't-leak fail-safe dual of untrusted-by-default (CF-2); **sticky, most-confidential-wins propagation** through every derivation so a paraphrase of a secret does not launder its sensitivity — the exact dual of most-untrusted-wins taint (CF-3); **every outbound sink declares a capacity and over-capacity flow is blocked**, generalizing the single SEC-3 device-edge egress gate to every sink (a tool call, a log, an outbound message, a prompt an untrusted party can read) at its own capacity (CF-4); **both axes required** since the dominant attack chains untrusted-in to secret-out and an injection defense with no exfiltration defense is routed around not stopped (CF-5); confidential content carried as an **opaque reference** so an agent routes a secret it never holds in a leakable surface (CF-6); declassification explicit/authorized/recorded, never implicit or self-granted, the dual of explicit trust elevation (CF-7, composes SEC-10); enforced by **mechanism at the sink, not by instructing the model** since a prompt 'don't leak' is defeatable by the very injection this defends against (CF-8, composes INT-5); inspectable and auditable with blocked and permitted flows recorded (CF-9); local-first with the label side-band metadata that never leaks the value it protects (CF-10). Nodus realization = l1-nodus-language NL-21 (the confidentiality dual of NL-11 provenance). Concept-only (tech-agnostic). v1.1.0: CF-11 (an opt-in diagnostics/telemetry egress channel is a machine-enforced field-level allowlist that fail-closes on unlisted keys, values as buckets/enums never free-text or identifiers, a dry-run that prints the exact payload, default-off in CI and source builds) | Stable | 1 | 1.1.0 |
| [l1-compensation.md](specifications/l1-compensation.md) | Effect compensation — the **third answer** to a committed-then-failed effect, the middle rung the recovery stack lacked between state rollback (crash-recovery, reversible internal state) and honest surfacing (conversation-rewind RW-5, irreversible and orphaned): **semantic undo by a declared forward inverse, never a state rollback** — a committed external effect (payment, provision, sent message) is cancelled in its *business meaning* by executing a different declared action (refund, deprovision, retract), not by restoring prior state which structurally cannot reach it, the two complementary and never conflated (CO-1); a per-step compensating action that is a **first-class fallible effect** passing the same gate, a step without one **honestly un-compensable** not silently assumed reversible (CO-2); only successfully-completed effects compensated, still-running work cancelled not compensated (CO-3); **reverse completion order (LIFO) as a correctness requirement** since later effects were built on earlier ones so forward-order undo acts on unremoved dependencies (CO-4); scoped to a declared compensation boundary, not reaching across unrelated scopes (CO-5); triggered by failure/cancellation/explicit request, **never automatic on success** where a completed scope keeps its effects (CO-6); bounded and its **own failure surfaced as an incomplete compensation, never swallowed**, since a failed compensation leaves the business effect live and reporting it undone converts a known problem into a hidden one (CO-7); idempotent and safe to re-drive after a crash mid-compensation (CO-8); recorded and auditable with the ledger showing both the effect and its countervailing compensation, never erasing history (CO-9); and an **honest net-effect statement** — what committed, what was compensated, what remains live — so a partially-compensated scope is never presented as cleanly undone, completing RW-5's surfacing with the remediation path it lacked (CO-10). Nodus realization = l1-nodus-language NL-22. Concept-only (tech-agnostic) | Stable | 1 | 1.0.0 |
| [l1-host-native-rendering.md](specifications/l1-host-native-rendering.md) | Host-native rendering — the **outbound** distribution contract (the mirror of `l1-extension-marketplace`'s inbound path): how a definition authored once here is materialized into a **foreign host's own native format**, expanding the shape AFS-14 only names. One authored source with derived, never-edited renditions (HNR-1) regenerable from source alone so no information lives only in a rendition (HNR-12, the DAH-1 derived-only-never-authority argument); **named render contracts** whose output is byte-identical for the same source+format+version, two targets sharing a format only if their artifacts are identical, non-determinism a defect because it makes renditions undiffable (HNR-2); **declared fidelity classes** — per-unit / collapsed / opaque — that consumers **branch on** rather than infer from a file extension (HNR-3), with the loss a class cannot carry **declared at plan time** and a collapsed rendition never reported in terms implying per-unit behavior it cannot deliver (HNR-4); **section roles as the portability contract** — identity-vs-operation planes split on declared roles never prose heuristics, and a definition that classifies into neither flagged at authoring time when the author is still present (HNR-5); **stable machine slug distinct from the drift-prone display name**, every reference pointing at the slug, collisions a build failure, renames a governed migration (HNR-6, twin of XM-2); declared detection evidence / install scopes / destination templates derived by **every** consumer from one catalog declaration per DC-1, a hardcoded second target list being the defect that silently drops targets (HNR-7); **plan before write** — previewable, idempotent, per-source attributable so removal is credible (HNR-8); explicit **snapshot vs live binding** mode, the two having different truth semantics (HNR-9); selection as a first-class catalog filter, load-bearing on collapsed targets (HNR-10); consented enumerable out-of-workspace materialization that moves instruction text and **never authority** (HNR-11). §4.5 applies PD-1/PD-5 **across the system boundary**: a large catalog renders as a small fixed surface plus an on-disk index rather than N eagerly-resident descriptors, since eager host catalogs spend the *receiving* system's context budget. Composes l1-agent-framework-skeleton (AFS-14) / l1-extension-marketplace (inbound twin) / l1-derived-artifact-handoff / l1-declarative-configuration (DC-1) / l1-progressive-disclosure / l1-deployment-neutrality / l1-corpus-originality (tech-agnostic). v1.1.0: HNR-11 consent tiered by blast radius (VCS'd repo files ⊃ machine-wide config ⊃ project-local un-tracked), separately declinable, no-consent-channel → write nothing + print; HNR-8 marker-fenced own region in a file the system doesn't own, removal deletes exactly what install wrote, durable opt-out a self-refresh must honour (cf. l1-rejection-memory) | Stable | 1 | 1.1.0 |
| [l1-corpus-originality.md](specifications/l1-corpus-originality.md) ; v1.1.0 ORI-12 — the duplicate search covers **withdrawn and closed** members (a match among them is a *regression signal*, worth more than the duplicate it resembles) and is keyed on the candidate's **identifying features rather than its own phrasing**, since a self-phrased query retrieves the neighbours of the author's wording and misses the near-duplicate that said the same thing differently; for a corpus another party maintains, a candidate adding nothing is withheld rather than submitted as agreement | Corpus originality — the **admission gate on sameness** for curated, selected-from corpora (roles, skills, archetypes, personas, templates, catalog entries), catching the one defect class every other gate is blind to: the **re-skin**, an admitted member re-published with its domain/platform/market nouns swapped, which is well-formed, accurate, individually defensible, and invisible to any reviewer who cannot hold the whole corpus in memory. Novelty as an admission criterion beside well-formedness, which is necessary and never sufficient (ORI-1); measured against **the corpus and the pending change set**, closing the two-identical-newcomers hole a corpus-only check cannot see (ORI-2); comparison over the substantive body with **find-replace-prone tokens neutralized** — proper nouns, platforms, markets, the unit's own name — since a comparison that treats a swapped noun as difference measures the disguise (ORI-3); **graded pass/warn/fail**, a single boundary forcing a choice between blocking legitimate neighbours and admitting re-skins (ORI-4); **thresholds calibrated against the corpus's own measured similarity distribution with a published baseline and stated margin** — a threshold with no published baseline is a guess wearing the authority of a number and gets deleted the first time it fires (ORI-5); the baseline **re-measured over time**, upward drift read as the corpus **converging** and answered by consolidation or codification, never by relaxing the gate into silence (ORI-6); findings that name the **nearest neighbour and the score** (ORI-7, TW-4 teach-or-be-suppressed); legitimate overlap resolved by substantive distinctness or a **declared** variant/specialization relationship that stores a delta instead of a second copy — never by threshold relaxation or blanket exemption (ORI-8, TW-7); **blocking to the agent, human-overridable with a recorded reason** (ORI-9, PC-2); **one mechanism at two moments** — admission gate plus whole-corpus convergence audit feeding MC-6 redundancy→merge, since a gate without the audit never repairs pre-existing duplication (ORI-10); and the stated cost model: the harm is **routing ambiguity** under PD-4 — two near-identical descriptors give a router no principled basis to choose — **not** storage or tidiness (ORI-11). §4.5 demarcates it from memory consolidation (observations, after the fact), AFS-13 generational novelty (a lineage in time, not a catalog at rest), and ROL-9 justification (reasons, not content) (tech-agnostic) | Stable | 1 | 1.1.0 |
| [l1-reasoning-spend.md](specifications/l1-reasoning-spend.md) | Reasoning spend — the accounting contract for **generation the principal pays for and never receives**: a deliberating model's internal reasoning, produced under the same call as the answer, billed at generation rates, consuming the same capacity, and on most providers never delivered at all. The system already owned the **dial** (`l1-generation-shaping` GS-3 depth modulation) and the **length budget** (`l1-generation-budget`) but never the **meter** between them, so a quantity routinely larger than the answer it precedes was implicitly folded into delivered output. A distinct unit class extending CR-2's per-class rates, justified by differing on delivery, amortization, governance, and reportability — a blended figure cannot answer *did the bill rise because answers got longer or because the model started thinking harder* (RSN-1); **shared capacity with deliberation produced first**, splitting GB-2's truncation signal into two causes with **opposite** remedies — answer-cut-off (escalate, continue from the partial) vs capacity-exhausted-before-the-answer (raise the cap or lower depth), where GB-3 continuation from a nonexistent partial silently re-spends the whole budget on deliberation again, the two separable by zero-delivered-output (RSN-2); three never-conflated visibility states content / count-only / **not reported**, the last a gap marker never a zero, since a turn that did not think and a turn whose thinking went unreported are the same number and opposite facts (RSN-3); **not amortized by prefix reuse** (IC-1 cheapens re-ingesting context, never thinking again), so a cost model projecting warmth-driven decay under-forecasts worst on the deepest turns (RSN-4); depth as a **declared fidelity axis** moving cost and quality together, making cross-depth figures incomparable without the depth (RSN-5); attributed under OAC-1 **especially on turns that produced nothing** — the case most likely to be dropped from a denominator and most distorting when it is, since dropping it makes an unproductive session look efficient (RSN-6); **separately reported under measurement** (MB-3 discipline), since equal answers with a tenfold deliberation gap are economically different (RSN-7); visible reasoning is untrusted model-authored **trace, never a record of action** — a claim is never verified against it, the acting record is the effect record (RSN-8); counted against the provider allowance so a readout has no unexplained falling balance (RSN-9); honest degradation as **declared lower bounds** when unmeasured, CR-4's fail-visible discipline applied to the **count** rather than the rate (RSN-10). §4.5 demarcates it from the system's own inner-monologue channel — separate budgets, and an inner-monologue call has its own reasoning spend under this contract. Composes generation-shaping / generation-budget / cost-rating / usage-allowance / outcome-attributed-cost / model-benchmarking / inference-cache / fidelity-variants / claim-verification; the nodus realization is `l1-nodus-environment` NE-15 (declared budget scope) | Stable | 1 | 1.0.0 |
| [l1-scoped-generalization.md](specifications/l1-scoped-generalization.md) | Scoped generalization — **where a learned pattern applies**, the axis the codification pathway never carried. PC-1/PC-4 measure recurrence across **time** and decide *bindingness*, so five observations on five days inside one project clear every existing gate and yield a project convention wearing the authority of a law; the leak is confident (indistinguishable at the point of use from an earned rule), invisible (nothing fails — the agent just works in the wrong style), compounding (mis-scoped rules shape the next observations), and self-discrediting (the user's conclusion is *the learning is unreliable*, and the well-earned rules get disabled along with it). Born narrow with an absent scope **unusable rather than universal** (SG-1); **durable context identity** surviving relocation and distinguishing coinciding things — HNR-6's argument applied to contexts, since an identity that is really a location orphans patterns when the thing moves and cross-contaminates when two things share a path (SG-2); **widening requires independent recurrence** — repetition *within* one context evidences the pattern is real, never that it generalizes, and independence is a **declared judgment** with a stated reason since two checkouts of one repository are one context (SG-3, the load-bearing invariant); widening a distinct **ratified** act recording which contexts supplied the evidence, never a counter crossing a threshold (SG-4); **contradiction narrows rather than excepts**, a wide rule with a growing exception list being one whose behavior nobody can predict (SG-5); scope checked **at application** and not only at storage, since a write-time filter leaves the corpus permanently contaminated by everything predating the model and by every import (SG-6); **imported patterns arrive narrow** regardless of the scope claimed at source — another corpus's *global* is evidence about their world and a hypothesis about yours (SG-7); cross-context leakage a **first-class defect worse than not learning**, detectable and reportable (SG-8); candidate decay so the pending pool never becomes a second unaudited rule corpus whose size later argues for bulk promotion (SG-9); scope explainable at the point of application, an unarguable rule being either obeyed blindly or disabled wholesale (SG-10); and **scope orthogonal to bindingness**, since collapsing the axes forces *important* to mean *everywhere* (SG-11). §4.5 demarcates from IMP-2 generalize-at-capture (what *leaves* vs how far what stays may *reach*). Prior worked instance recorded: nodus LP-3's two-independent-host rule is SG-3 for the library-extraction case | Stable | 1 | 1.0.0 |
| [l1-output-repair.md](specifications/l1-output-repair.md) | Output repair — the **deterministic post-generation correction layer** every model-consuming system grows and almost every one grows *anonymously*: a `.replace()` in a parser fixing leaked tool-call syntax emitted as prose, an internal field escaped into user text, a misplaced marker, an unparseable envelope. The repairs are legitimate; what is missing is that each one is **evidence** — a claim that a specific model misbehaves in a specific way, whose firing rate answers questions nothing else does. Named declared repairs with no anonymous fixups, since an unnamed correction cannot be counted, tested at its trigger, retired, or attributed (RPR-1); **form only, never meaning** — a transformation that changes what the output *says* is an unreviewed edit by a component with no authority to make it (RPR-2); deterministic, idempotent, order-declared (RPR-3); **every firing counted and attributed to (repair, provider, model, version)**, since an uncounted repair converts a measurable model defect into invisible maintenance and makes *did the new model get better* unanswerable by construction (RPR-4); **both rate extremes actionable** — never-fires is dead code encoding an unchecked guarantee, always-fires is an upstream prompt/contract defect being papered over while its evidence is hidden (RPR-5, NEG-10 discipline); **silent to the model, never silent to the record**, because a corrected output indistinguishable from a correct one destroys exactly the information the layer produces (RPR-6); **no streaming counterpart means no protection on the streaming path** — a repair written over a complete string silently does not apply to the incremental path the product usually ships, so the streaming form is declared or the gap is (RPR-7); repair for decidable **form**, contract validate-and-retry for **semantics**, where repairing a semantic defect is fabrication and retrying a form defect is waste (RPR-8); **repairs as model-scoped hypotheses** recording the versions they were observed against and re-opened on a model change, since a stale repair corrupts output a new model produces legitimately (RPR-9, SG-3 argument); **a repair never widens trust or promotes content to control** — a helpful reconstruction of a malformed control token is the injection TB-5 exists to prevent (RPR-10). §4.4 reconciles silent form-editing with CV-6's never-silently-edits by locating the boundary at meaning; §4.5 nodus disposition: no new invariant, the layer is host-side behind the model-provider seam | Stable | 1 | 1.0.0 |
| [l1-computed-grounding.md](specifications/l1-computed-grounding.md) | Computed grounding — **the model narrates, the code decides**: where a request's factual core is mechanically computable from state the system already holds (which of my things are in state X, how many, what total, when last), the system computes it and the model is reduced to **expressing** it. Asking a model to derive a filter-and-count from serialized context when the exact operation is one call away is a design defect, not a prompting problem — and the failure has an unusually trust-destroying shape because the wrong answer is *specific*, inviting belief rather than checking (CGR-1); the computed result enters as a constraint that **states its own authority**, since a computed value offered as mere context is one input among many and loses to a plausible derivation, and it must say exactly what to reproduce with nothing added or dropped (CGR-2); **narrow, conservative, observable detection** — a false fire is **worse than no grounding**, forcing a confidently wrong answer carrying full authority, so the detector is tuned against that asymmetry rather than for coverage, and the pressure to widen always arrives as a report about a miss and never about the invisible false fire (CGR-3); computed from the **same snapshot** the model reads, or the constraint contradicts the context and forces a choice between two authorities (CGR-4); **empty as a first-class answer**, the case a model handed a question is most tempted to fill (CGR-5); **extension fixed, expression free** — the split that makes the same question in another language return the same set while keeping the answer from degenerating into a table (CGR-6); ungrounded as a **declared** degradation, since a silent downgrade turns a known reliability boundary into an unknown one (CGR-7); **prevention and verification both kept** — grounding is narrow and nearly free, claim-verification is general and pays for the wrong answer before catching it, so neither may be dropped for the other (CGR-8); grounded-ness inspectable, without which neither the model's real accuracy nor the layer's coverage is measurable (CGR-9). The generation-side sibling of OL-7's exact-grounding-not-similarity-recall; §4.5 nodus disposition: no new invariant, a computed value is an ordinary bound input | Stable | 1 | 1.0.0 |
| [l2-tool-receipts.md](specifications/l2-tool-receipts.md) | Implements l1-tool-receipts. The realization of model-unforgeable per-action execution receipts as a **keyed-BLAKE3 MAC over a length-prefixed action binding**, closing a gap found and disclosed during the developer-office build rather than hidden. Zero new crates — `blake3` is already a `cronus-domain` dependency and already on the domain boundary-guard allowlist, which is what makes TR-1's unconditional coverage affordable. Domain/facade tier split on the established `*_bootstrap.rs` precedent: deterministic `ReceiptKey`/`ActionBinding`/`mint`/`verify` in `crates/domain/src/tool_receipts.rs`, OS entropy + dispatch wiring in `crates/core/src/receipts_bootstrap.rs`. **Corrects a real hazard in the parent's reference sketch**: literal concatenation is forgeable by field-boundary shifting, so the binding is length-prefixed and injective, domain-separated, with a constant-time tag compare (TR-2/TR-3). TR-1 held **structurally** — `Receipted<T>` has no public constructor and `ReceiptedDispatch::invoke` is the only execution path, so a receipt-free path does not compile (the BA-4/OA-4/DVO-3 lineage applied to execution authenticity, and what an append-to-a-log baseline can never provide). TR-4 realized as a **default-deny on the fact-recording path** via `ReceiptLedger::status`, explicitly not prose parsing: no API exists that upgrades an unreceipted claim to fact. TR-8's `Pending` is a distinct state, never a receipt over a placeholder result — that would be a valid receipt for a false claim. TR-5's key is ephemeral, non-`Debug`/`Serialize`, zeroed on drop, with the volatile-write residual stated honestly; TR-9 strengthens the existing SEC-7 audit log and records a verify mismatch as its own auditable event. nodus: no new primitive — HO-9 stores and echoes an opaque host token, never mints | Stable | 2 | 1.0.0 |

One registry with per-actor layers, never a registry per actor, since forking multiplies the surface by the staff count and drifts on the first amendment (SCL-1); the registration site drives **visibility and lifetime as one fact**, making "visible here, disposed there" unrepresentable (SCL-2); one parent chain read in **opposite directions** — supply inherits down, observation admits up — because a composition supplies what it composed and observes activity from it (SCL-3); nearest-wins shadowing as **replacement, never merge**, a surface assembled from halves of two authored things having no author (SCL-4); governing contributions read **exact-scope**, since inherited governance is a decision nobody made and transitivity belongs to the guard plane INT-5 (SCL-5); restrictions narrowing the shared set by **intersection**, scope-local merging after, so restriction and contribution never race (SCL-6); a filtered-away capability **absent from the surface and refused on invocation**, both halves required (SCL-7); a bounded **setup window** closing before publication whose occupant registers but never drives, with whole-creation rollback (SCL-8); scoping declared **not** an authority boundary, stated in the contract because a mechanism shaped like isolation is used as one unless it says otherwise (SCL-9); isolation claimed only by planes that declare they file by scope (SCL-10); reachability inherited from the minter and narrowed by minting narrower (SCL-11); per-plane default membership declared, an undeclared default failing as silent over-capability (SCL-12); lineage carried as **data** so a topology change is never a capability change (SCL-13) | Stable | 1 | 1.0.0 |
| [l1-composition-binding.md](specifications/l1-composition-binding.md) | Composition binding — the **runtime half** the role/archetype catalog specs never covered: what happens when a composition file is edited while sessions run on it. A composition mounts **once** and is joined by many (CBD-1); a binding names a **generation**, and a joined generation outlives its source being edited or deleted, because an actor whose world follows its file has a history its own record cannot explain (CBD-2); a child joins its parent's exact generation **by reference**, never re-resolving the name — re-resolution hands it a different generation after an edit and fails it outright after a delete while the parent runs on (CBD-3); **started-with** is a frozen creation fact and **runs-on** is resolved by every reconstruction path, since reading the header rebuilds a switched actor under a composition that cannot account for its own recorded actions (CBD-4); a rebind is a durable event appended after it commits (CBD-5); rebinding permitted only while the actor has produced nothing, checked where the history is in hand (CBD-6); rebind atomic and restoring, two compositions never coexisting (CBD-7); **two dispositions** for an unusable artifact — an unclaimable identity skipped, unloadable content *listed with its reason*, because a skipped artifact still occupies its identity while every surface shows nothing to delete (CBD-8); discovery re-read rather than memoized (CBD-9); **copy-only authoring**, which can mint neither an unloadable artifact nor capability (CBD-10); containment as a property of the **identity** rather than a later path check (CBD-11); display metadata forbidden from claiming identity or trust (CBD-12); declared per-kind resolution anchors (CBD-13) | Stable | 1 | 1.0.0 |
| [l1-composition-layering.md](specifications/l1-composition-layering.md) ; v1.1.0 LAY-11 — a layer that **fails to load is a failed layer, never an empty one**: the third member of the LAY-4/LAY-5 family at the layer grain, and the one where the failure is invisible, since the stack below still composes into a valid effective set and the only symptom is that everything the author declared is missing — which reads as never having written it; disposition declared (block per LAY-10, or exclude with a standing visible defect), never a quiet drop | Composition layering — assembling the **set of components** from ordered layers; the set-valued sibling of `l1-declarative-configuration` DC-11's field-valued resolution, demarcated in §4.6. Author-assigned stable entry identity as the **sole** reconciliation basis, since a generated identity turns every edit into remove-plus-add and restarts components nobody changed — a correctness-preserving failure nobody can attribute (LAY-1); **whole replacement, no deep merge**, because merge semantics for absent keys, explicit nulls, and collection order are decided by an algorithm no layer author reads (LAY-2); disabled as a durable state of the entry rather than its removal, present-and-off and absent being different facts with different remedies (LAY-3); an override matching nothing **reported**, since typo and base-changed-underneath are both actionable and present identically (LAY-4); empty declaration distinguished from absent, a parse-to-nothing being an error because a blanked file is more often a mistake than a statement (LAY-5); **one** composition algorithm serving the run path and every inspection surface, two implementations agreeing until the moment someone needs the report (LAY-6); a distributed unit contributing into the stack so its insertions stay overridable from above (LAY-7); ownership decided **structurally** — exact match to the shipped form is system-owned, any deviation user-owned and untouched — a stored "modified" flag being a second source of truth wrong in both directions (LAY-8); declared total layer order (LAY-9); transactional recomposition leaving the last good set running on failure (LAY-10) | Stable | 1 | 1.1.0 |
| [l1-execution-locus.md](specifications/l1-execution-locus.md) ; v1.1.1 links-only - added l1-remote-liveness-verdict to Related Specifications: RLV-8 states the same-underlying-resource-one-registration rule this spec's substitution model assumes, and RLV supplies what a locus's liveness capability returns once its channel (not its capability set) becomes unavailable; no invariant changed | Execution locus — the previously-unmodeled **execution-host dimension**: one coherent world (filesystem, process table, network position, acting identity), with **the locus as the unit of substitution, never the individual capability**. Every world-touching capability declares its locus and none assumes the host (LOC-1); co-resident capabilities move **as a set**, since a shell in one world and a file reader in another yield tools that individually succeed and jointly describe an impossible world — a failure that errors nowhere, spends the agent's turns, and produces a confidently wrong account (LOC-2); handles are locus-bound and crossing is an explicit transfer (LOC-3); moving the locus must not **fork the consumers**, which is the leverage — one substitution, whole-product effect, no remote variants of shell/file/terminal/indexer (LOC-4); the active locus is observable and **stamped** on every artifact claiming something about a world, an unstamped observation being evidence about nowhere (LOC-5); a capability absent from the active locus refuses visibly and **never falls back**, a host fallback recreating the split world at the exact seam the locus closed (LOC-6); confinement composes with a locus and never defines one, conflating them producing the unmade claim that remote is safe (LOC-7); locus selection is a **composition** decision, not a per-call argument that hands the choice of where effects land to the least accountable participant (LOC-8); world-derived state keyed by locus and invalidated or explicitly carried, which is what stops a stale local index answering confidently about a remote world (LOC-9); a locus declares the operational properties that change how it must be used (LOC-10). Demarcated from `l1-execution-sandbox` (how confined) and `l1-environment-lifecycle` (does the instance exist) ; v1.1.0 LOC-11/LOC-12 selection across many surfaces — **one decider per application** delivers the default locus to every surface (N surfaces deriving it drift the moment inputs change at different times, giving the product several simultaneous internally-consistent opinions about where work runs), changed by exactly one gesture; and two declared binding disciplines — a **preference** resolves to its target while it can serve and to the default while it cannot, auto-following and returning on its own (safe because it holds no locus-bound state), while a **binding** is fixed for its container's lifetime because the container holds LOC-3 handles, so relocation is **clone-not-migrate** and availability is established at creation rather than polled (§4.6) | Stable | 1 | 1.1.1 |
| [l1-peer-compatibility.md](specifications/l1-peer-compatibility.md) ; v1.1.0 PCO-15 added - a **content or meaning change on a structurally unchanged surface** is a compatibility change PCO-5's structural fingerprint cannot see by construction: a field the emitter quietly stops populating, a value whose meaning/units/nullability shifts with no type change, or a message that starts/stops flowing on an existing path with no new opcode produce **no structural diff to key on**, and PCO-7's parse-tolerance rule doesn't apply either since the field is present and well-typed. Declared **on the contract itself** as a reviewed claim, the same way PCO-11 declares emitter behavior no structural diff can see; undeclared, the change is breaking and silence is never read as the compatible case | Peer compatibility under **version skew** — the contract between two independently released halves of one product (core↔frontend, engine↔mobile client, office↔remote office, host↔extension, runtime↔workflow bundle) that are never guaranteed to be the same age. Skew is the normal operating state, not a fault, and compatibility must be *implemented and machine-verified* rather than asserted in a changelog. Contract, distribution and persistence versions are three independent systems (PCO-1); the surface is a set of **independently versioned capability lines**, never one protocol version that lets the most volatile capability set the floor for all (PCO-2); **the newer peer adapts and the older peer never learns**, which bounds each peer's obligation to its own history instead of the population — three of five negotiation arms are yes-because-I-am-older (PCO-3); compatibility is a declared **executable transform**, so a hole in the chain is an incompatibility not a documentation gap (PCO-4); the version label is verified against a normalized structural fingerprint, removing the author's ability to adjudicate their own diff (PCO-5); **direction decides severity** — the same value-set growth is safe inbound and fatal outbound, because the older peer decodes what it is handed unconditionally (PCO-6); **parse tolerance is not compatibility** — optionality proves the parse succeeds and nothing about whether the payload carries the key or whether every consumer runs the parse (PCO-7); a fail-closed **floor** plus an **optional** surface degrading per capability as *unsupported* or as an adapted *fallback*, with new capabilities barred from the floor (PCO-8); the oracle compares against surfaces captured from **released artifacts**, immutable and never edited to make a check pass (PCO-9); severities copied from the transport's observed failure behaviour (PCO-10); emitter properties no fingerprint can see recorded as reviewed declarations, silence never read as the claim (PCO-11); exceptions as identifier-agnostic standing policy, never per-change sign-off (PCO-12); a static mirror pinned to the runtime oracle by test (PCO-13); and a verdict naming capability, both versions and **which side to upgrade**, derived per capability (PCO-14).
